### 2026-10-11 be-framework -> fe-framework, orchestrator, ui-ux-designer, integration-test-engineer, be-dba (supervisor re-test: backend findings PR-04, PR-05, PR-08, PR-10, PR-11, PS-12, and the QA reviews of the first version)
- Produced (branch `feature/qa-backend-fixes-5`, supersedes PR 46 `feature/avg-cost-and-money-precision-3`; the name `-4` was taken by an old merged branch on the remote): PR-04, PR-05, PR-08, PR-10, PR-11, PS-12 and the status list `docs/qa/fix-status-backend-2.md`; migration `AddDocumentReversedBy`. PS-14 (the balance after a ledger row) is in its own pull request, `feature/ledger-balance-after`, because it rewrites every ledger row. The average-cost and money-precision work is NOT here (PR 44, PR 45). Details and all 1M-row numbers: `docs/architecture/data-and-performance-rules.md` section 9.
- What the reviews asked and what was done: **M1** a partner-name search is never cut off: up to 50 matching partners are tested as an id list, more through a join, both with the same lower-cased binary comparison as the number and the remark (so the result does not depend on the count); tests with 50, 51 and 350 partners and a partner named "AESOP". **M2** measured on 1,001,800 documents: the cost of a contains-match is the CPU of the case-insensitive comparison under the linguistic collation, so the fix is the lower-cased binary comparison (`EfDocumentTextMatcher`: 3.1 s to 0.41 s of CPU for a term that matches nothing); the narrow index `IX_StockDocument_Search` only cut the logical reads of the count 2.4x for 86 MB and a wider write, so it is removed. **Never stocked** (second review, replaces the per-warehouse rule of the first fix): a product with a safety level and no balance row in ANY active warehouse the caller can see is listed ONCE (quantity 0, no warehouse); products with a balance row follow the per-warehouse rule; only the caller's warehouses count. **Minor**: `StockRowsSpec` takes `isLow` from `LowStockRule.Project`, `LowStockSets` builds the sets for both readers; low means on-hand AT OR BELOW the safety level; `MarkReversed` requires a user id above zero; `IX_StockDocument_ReversedBy` is filtered (`WHERE [ReversedBy] IS NOT NULL`); the migration states the enum values its backfill uses and `MigrationTests` ties them to the enums and runs the migration up, down, up with data; the movements export has "Reversal" as its last column; `WarehouseScopeTests` reads every page; the name-order test no longer depends on the server collation; the money rows and section are out of `fix-status-backend-2.md`.
- Honest numbers (1M documents, shared laptop, p50): a text search WITH the total (the default) takes 2 to 4 s (no match 2.2 s, rare term 1.4 s, "delivery" 1.06 s, 11 partners 2.5 s, 541 partners 4.5 s; scoped to one warehouse, rare term 3.4 s); WITHOUT the total 0.73 s for a term that matches nothing and under 0.1 s for a term with many matches (scoped: 1.23 s no match, 1.38 s rare term). The partner arm makes the count three to four times dearer. That default is NOT under the 1 s target; it is a documented follow-up, not fixed here. The low-stock list at 172,906 balance rows: count 650 ms of CPU, page 1.7 s at any depth (admin; 18,321 rows for a user scoped to one warehouse, count 0.70 s, page 0.48 s wall); accepted with these figures (the options to cheapen it are in section 9).
- Needs from fe-framework: adapt `web/` to the section below and regenerate the client (`StockSort.Name`, `DocumentDto.reversedBy/reversedByName/reversedAt`, the `hideSignIns` audit parameter, `StockRow` and `LowStockAlert` warehouse fields nullable, `StockRow.avgCost/value` null on never-stocked rows). For a text search send `includeTotal=false` and page with Previous/Next, and block a search under 3 characters in the box ("Type at least 3 characters"): the API answers it with a 400. A low-stock row without a warehouse is a product stocked nowhere: show "no stock in any warehouse" and a receipt shortcut that asks for the warehouse.
- Needs from ui-ux-designer / product owner: the decisions at the end of `docs/qa/fix-status-backend-2.md` (Q3 Cancel on an Approved document, cost of stock received by Warehouse staff, date format, paging controls, dialog focus); whether a text search should require or default a date range at larger sizes.
- Needs from be-dba: the index set changed from the first version of PR 46: `IX_StockDocument_Search` is gone, `IX_StockDocument_ReversedBy` is filtered. A dev database that already applied the first PR 46 migration (`DocumentSearchIndexAndReversedBy`) or the first version of this one must go back first: `dotnet ef database update AddDocumentReversedAt` (or drop the `ReversedBy` column, its index and its history row by hand).
- Needs from integration-test-engineer: nothing; new suites are `LowStockNeverStockedTests`, `MigrationTests` and new cases in `DocumentSearchTests`, `LowStockOneDefinitionTests`, `WarehouseScopeTests`, `SignedStockEffectTests`.
- Decisions others must respect: (1) **Low stock is one rule, `Ims.Domain.LowStockRule`, and it follows the design document: on-hand `<=` the safety level, negative always low** (the first version said "equal is not low"). Only active products in ACTIVE warehouses count. A new place that asks "is it low" uses the rule, never its own condition. (2) **Never stocked = no balance row in any active warehouse the caller can see; listed once, no warehouse.** This replaces the earlier part-3 decision ("never stocked in ANY warehouse" of the whole system, so a scoped caller's answer depended on warehouses they could not see) and the first fix of this branch (once per warehouse, which made the list products x warehouses). (3) A text search needs 3 characters (400 otherwise). (4) The movements Excel export has the "Reversal" column LAST (headers: Posted (MYT), Document, Type, Status, Warehouse, Partner, SKU, Product, Qty, Unit, Base qty, Stock effect, Unit cost, Amount, Reversal). (5) `ReversedBy` stays empty, and the document shows "Reversed" without a name, for a reversed document that has no reversal entry in the ledger and for a document reversed by the old code during a rolling deploy; the backfill does not guess. (6) Merge order with PR 45 and PR 52: the model snapshot conflicts mechanically; keep every migration in timestamp order, regenerate the snapshot and run `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes`.
- Open conflicts: none. Observation for fe-framework: the editor reloads `lines[].unitCost` (a cost per BASE unit) as the cost per entered unit, which is wrong for a line in a non-base unit; see the end of `fix-status-backend-2.md`.

#### Contract changes for the frontend (re-test round, this PR)
- **Excel.** The movements export has a new last column "Reversal" ("Yes" on the reversal row of a reversed document, blank otherwise).
- **`GET /api/stock` and `GET /api/reports/stock-summary/export`.** `lowOnly=true` is the low-stock definition: it also lists active products with a safety level that have no stock in any warehouse the caller can see, ONCE each: `warehouseId`, `warehouseCode` and `warehouseName` null, `qtyOnHand` 0, `avgCost` and `value` null, `isLow` true. A warehouse filter leaves them out. Its `total` equals `GET /api/stock/low-stock`. A quantity equal to the safety level IS low now. `isLow` is false for an inactive product and in an inactive warehouse. New `sortBy=Name` (the default is still `Sku`; the design wants the screen to open sorted by product name). Every sort is total (ends in SKU and warehouse code). The three warehouse fields of `StockRow` are nullable in the schema.
- **`GET /api/stock/low-stock`.** Worst first by the share missing from the safety level (then units, SKU, warehouse code); was by units. `warehouseId`, `warehouseCode`, `warehouseName` are nullable (a product stocked nowhere visible); `summary.lowCount` is the same number.
- **`GET /api/documents?search=`.** Matches the document number, the remark and the partner name, ignoring case (was the number only). A text of fewer than 3 characters (after trimming) is a 400 "Type at least 3 characters." Add `includeTotal=false` for a text search. `/api/documents/{id}` and the list unchanged otherwise.
- **`DocumentDto`.** New `reversedBy`, `reversedByName`, `reversedAt` (null until reversed; the name can stay null, see decision 5) for "Reversed by {name} on {date}".
- **`GET /api/audit`.** New `hideSignIns=true` (leaves out `login.success`, `login.failed`, `login.locked`, `login.lockout`, `logout`). A settings save now writes one `settings.updated` entry (entity `SystemSetting`, id `all`) with the old and new values of all settings; the per-key `updated` rows no longer exist.
- Unchanged: the permissions, `allowedActions`, the reversal-blocked answer, the paging contract, the If-Match rules.

### 2026-10-11 be-framework -> fe-framework, be-dba, integration-test-engineer, orchestrator (PS-14: the balance after each ledger row, stored)
- Produced (branch `feature/ledger-balance-after`; split out of the QA fixes of PR 46/51 because it rewrites every ledger row): `LedgerRow.BalanceAfter` on `GET /api/stock/ledger?productId=...`, stored on `StockLedgerEntry.BalanceAfter` (`decimal(18,4)`) when the posting or the reversal inserts the entry (each posting strategy passes the balance after the line to `PostingContext.AddEntry`), under the same per-(warehouse, product) lock and in the same transaction as the balance it follows. Migration `AddLedgerBalanceAfter` (column added empty, a running-sum backfill of the old rows with the append-only trigger switched off for that one statement, then NOT NULL; no default, so a stale writer fails loudly). Tests: `LedgerBalanceAfterTests` (running sums, page sizes and cursor, reversals, two warehouses, one document, scope, 24 parallel postings, every ledger row of the test database), `LedgerBalanceAfterEntryTests` (domain), `LedgerBalanceMigrationTests` (backfill, trigger back on, down, up with data).
- Why stored: measured on a ledger of 1,499,484 rows with one product of 500,020 rows, deriving it (current balance minus everything booked after the page) read 3,492 pages for a page in the middle and 20,506 for the last page (700-780 ms of CPU) on top of the page; an index `(ProductId, WarehouseId, Id) INCLUDE (QtyDelta)` is 45 MB (28 % of the table, written by every posting) and only halves that; stored costs 9 bytes a row and the page reads 9 pages at any depth. It also cannot be overtaken by a concurrent posting. Numbers and the maintenance window: `docs/architecture/data-and-performance-rules.md` section 9.
- Needs from be-dba: run `AddLedgerBalanceAfter` in a maintenance window: it updates every ledger row once (62 s for 1.5M rows on a loaded laptop, about a minute per million), holds a schema lock on `StockLedgerEntry`, and leaves the table twice the size and 98 % fragmented; rebuild only `PK_StockLedgerEntry` straight after (the whole-table rebuild took 38 s here, back to 154 MB). Estimates: about 0.5 KB of transaction log per row (about 3 GB at 6M rows, full recovery model) and about 200 MB of tempdb at 6M rows for the window sort. `docs/dba/08-reconcile-drill.sql` now checks the stored values and `01-seed-perf.sql` fills them. Checked after the backfill: 0 differences between the last `BalanceAfter` of every (product, warehouse) and `StockBalance`.
- Needs from fe-framework: regenerate the client (`LedgerRow.balanceAfter`, a number in base units, null when no `productId` is given) and show it on the product ledger table (design 3.7 step 4).
- Decisions others must respect: (1) the ledger is still never updated except by the one backfill in this migration. (2) Anything that creates ledger entries must give `PostingContext.AddEntry` the balance after the movement (the four posting strategies do). (3) The migration sorts after `20261011072009_AddDocumentReversedBy` (PR 51) and after PR 44's and 45's; if another migration lands first, rename this one later and run `dotnet ef migrations has-pending-model-changes`.
- Open conflicts: none.

### 2026-10-12 fe-framework -> qa-edge-accessibility, orchestrator (slice R04: money format and input rules, quantity rule ER-12)
- Produced (branch `feature/refresh-r04-money-format`): `maxDecimals(n)` moved to `shared-utils` (`forms/decimals.validator.ts`, now also reads an exponent such as 1e-7), `decimalPlaces` and `roundMoney` (half away from zero, like the API) in `shared-utils/numbers`; `appMoney` and `appUnitPrice` render nothing for a missing amount (D1); money inputs (receipt-line unit cost, approval-rule "Amount from") reject a 3rd decimal with "Enter an amount with at most 2 decimals." (en, zh, ms) and `step="0.01"`; the "(RM)" suffix on the labels doc.unitCost, doc.amount, stock.value, home.stockValue, reports.unitCost, rule.amountThreshold (D2); the approval-trigger sentence in the document editor uses `appMoney` and `appQty` (it had its own 3-decimal quantity format).
- ER-12: quantity minimum is 0.0001 (`MIN_LINE_QTY`), decimals rule is checked before the minimum so 0.00001 says "Use at most 4 decimals." and 0 or a negative says "Enter a quantity above 0."; a line the form rejects (quantity, or a unit cost with 3 decimals or negative) is left out of the totals line (`DocumentTotals.validLineCount`; `lineCount` still feeds the "Items (n)" heading). The receipt total is now the sum of per-line `round(qty x unit cost, 2)` as the API does (before: one rounding of the raw sum, which could differ by a cent).
- Beyond the plan, one small addition: `app-input type=number` reports `badInput` when the browser holds text it cannot read ("1,234.50" pasted, a lone "-"), shows "Enter a number such as 1234.50, without commas or spaces." and no longer clears that text on blur. Before, such a paste became an empty value without a word (an empty unit cost means "use the average cost"). The `validation.decimals` sentence now takes `{max}`.
- Tests: new specs for the validator, `decimalPlaces`/`roundMoney`, pipes in en, zh, ms, the input `badInput` rule, ui-text messages, the line rules and totals, the document editor (3rd decimal, ER-12) and the settings rule form (first spec in that lib); `e2e/tests/13-cost-visibility.spec.ts` extended (Clerk unit cost rejects 12.345) and its "(RM per base unit)" header updated; the e2e was not run (needs the API and database). Full web suite (35 projects), lint, lint:styles and the development build pass.
- Needs from qa-edge-accessibility: re-check ER-12 and the input errors on a phone (the message shows when the field is left or Save is pressed, not while typing). Decisions others must respect: money inputs use `maxDecimals(MONEY_DECIMALS)` and the `decimals` error key; the sentence is chosen by the limit (2 = money). Not done: qtyThreshold has no decimals rule; negative money keeps its ASCII minus; no change to cost visibility.

### 2026-10-12 be-framework -> fe-framework, behavior-test-engineer, be-dba, be-architect, orchestrator (GUID PR-2: opaque cursors)
- Produced (branch `feature/guid-pr2-opaque-cursors`): `CursorToken` (`src/Ims.Application/Common/CursorToken.cs`: base64url of a version byte and the 8-byte position, `Limits.CursorMaxLength` = 32, `Encode`, `TryDecode`, `Decode`), the `cursor` request parameter (string) and `nextCursor` (string or null) on the three seek-paged lists: `GET /api/documents`, `GET /api/stock/ledger`, `GET /api/audit`. `GET /api/reports/ledger/export` shares `LedgerFilter`, so its OpenAPI gains `cursor` too; the export ignores it, as it ignored `afterId`. Decoding yields the same `Id < position` predicate, so plans are unchanged; the order still ends in the internal `Id`. A cursor the server did not make (garbage, truncated, another version, over 32 characters, position below 1) is a 400 with `errors.cursor` (validator rule `MustBeAValidCursor` in the three list validators), never a 500. `Paging.NextCursor` and `PagedResult.NextCursor` carry the string.
- Clean switch, no compatibility shim: ADR 3.2 item 3 says the web does not use cursors. The old `afterId` is now an unknown query parameter and is silently ignored (the list restarts at page 1), so every script that pages must change. Done here: `PageWalk` (one constant), `PagingContractTests`, `LedgerBalanceAfterTests`, `tests/smoke/smoke.mjs` (cursor check plus a garbage-cursor 400), `tests/load/scenarios.js` (a 6-line `cursorAt(position)` helper builds a cursor the way the server does, verified against `CursorToken.Encode`; the id arithmetic stays until PR-2b samples ids in `setup()`). `e2e/` and `web/e2e` have no cursor use. New tests: `Ims.Domain.Tests/Common/CursorTokenTests` (round trip, URL-safe, key not visible, garbage, truncated, extended, other version, position below 1, 1 MB input, wire format pinned), and `PagingContractTests` (next cursor is the encoded last id, bad cursor is a 400 on `cursor` for all three lists).
- Needs from fe-framework (client batch CS, `npm run api:generate`, not here): regenerate; `nextCursor` becomes `string | undefined` and the query parameter `afterId?: number` becomes `cursor?: string` in the same position on `ledger`, `exportLedger`, audit `list` and documents `list`. No `web/libs` code passes or reads either (checked: no `afterId`/`nextCursor` use outside the generated client), so nothing else changes. `docs/architecture/frontend.md` line 46 still says "Seek paging (`afterId`)": change to `cursor`.
- Needs from be-dba: `docs/dba/02-measure-api.ps1` lines 83 and 99 use `afterId=` (deep seek measurements) and would now measure page 1; they need `cursor=<CursorToken of the id>` (same construction as `cursorAt` in `tests/load/scenarios.js`). `docs/dba/README.md` lines 38 and 94 are historical results, left as written.
- Needs from behavior-test-engineer (PR-2b): the k6 `setup()` id sampling (the GUID-plan part); `cursorAt` is a stop-gap for the 1M-tier run.
- Decisions others must respect: the cursor is opaque by contract; only tests and load scripts may construct one, via `CursorToken.Encode` or `cursorAt`, and nothing parses it. It is unsigned (ADR 2.4): if D2 turns out "yes" it moves to Data Protection and `cursorAt` is replaced by walking pages in `setup()`. `docs/architecture/data-and-performance-rules.md` section 8 documents the contract (small edit in be-architect's file; be-architect please confirm).
- Open conflicts: none. Run: `dotnet build -c Release -warnaserror --no-incremental` clean; Ims.Domain.Tests 753 passed, Ims.Architecture.Tests 68 passed; Ims.Api.Tests 1109 passed (3 new, 3 changed).
### 2026-10-12 be-framework -> fe-framework, be-dba, behavior-test-engineer, orchestrator (GUID PR-2 revised after review: cursors are ENCRYPTED)
- Supersedes the earlier PR-2 entry where it says the cursor is unsigned and "if D2 turns out yes": D2 is yes, so the cursor is encrypted in this PR. `ICursorProtector` (Application) is implemented by `DataProtectionCursorProtector` (`src/Ims.Api/Common`, ASP.NET Core Data Protection, purpose `Ims.Cursor.v1` plus one sub-purpose per list from `CursorLists`). `CursorToken` encodes version byte + `Id`, the protector encrypts and authenticates, the output is base64url. A cursor of another list, another purpose or key, altered, truncated, a variant spelling (whitespace, padding, standard base64), over `Limits.CursorMaxLength` (256, checked before decoding) or another version is a 400 on `errors.cursor`. The three handlers decode the cursor and hand the position to the specs; the validator rule is gone. Query plans are unchanged.
- Ops (hosting is undecided): the Data Protection key ring must be persisted and shared by all instances (a volume or shared store; in a container the default is ephemeral). A lost key ring, or a key past its lifetime, only makes outstanding cursors fail with a 400; the client restarts the walk from page 1. Acceptable, no data is lost. The same key ring is used by nothing else today.
- Release note: `afterId` is removed and silently ignored (page 1 is returned), a third-party client that loops on `nextCursor` under the old name would never advance. No known external client. The export endpoint (`GET /api/reports/ledger/export`) shares `LedgerFilter`, so its OpenAPI advertises `cursor` but ignores it (as before with `afterId`); a dedicated export filter was not worth it here.
- Tests and scripts: nobody builds a cursor any more; they walk pages. `CursorTokenTests` moved to `Ims.Api.Tests/Tests/Paging` (needs Data Protection): round trip, altered character at every position, another list, another purpose, another key, another version, position below 1, wrong length, garbage, truncated, extended, variant spellings, 1 MB input, and an opacity test. `tests/load/scenarios.js` collects cursors in `setup()` from the `nextCursor` of OFFSET pages up to the 10,000-row limit (no `cursorAt`, no id arithmetic for cursors). `docs/dba/02-measure-api.ps1` does the same: rows renamed `ledger: deep cursor (after row 10,000, x500)` and `documents: deep cursor (after row 10,000, x25)` (the old "50% in" rows are history in the csv files; a seek costs the same at any depth).
- Needs from fe-framework: unchanged (regenerate in the CS batch: `nextCursor` string, `afterId?: number` becomes `cursor?: string`; `frontend.md` line 46). Needs from behavior-test-engineer (PR-2b): k6 id sampling for the by-id scenarios only; cursors are done.
- Open conflicts: none.
### 2026-10-12 be-framework -> be-architect, be-dba, security-engineer, orchestrator (GUID PR-2 second review: the cursor key ring is configured)
- Produced: `src/Ims.Api/Composition/CursorProtectionSetup.cs` (replaces the bare `AddDataProtection()`): application name `Ims`, keys persisted to the directory in `DataProtection:KeysPath`. Production without the setting refuses to start; outside Production the default is `ims-dataprotection-keys` under the temp directory (works on Windows and Linux, never inside the repo). `Program.cs` protects one value at start-up (`VerifyCursorProtection`), so an unwritable directory stops the boot with a message naming the setting; before, the first full page of documents, ledger or audit would have been a 500 under `read_only: true`.
- Ops requirement (hosting is undecided): every instance must use the same persisted directory. Dockerfile: `/app/keys` created and owned by `$APP_UID`, `DataProtection__KeysPath=/app/keys`; docker-compose: `DataProtection__KeysPath`, named volume `keys` mounted at `/app/keys` (writable under `read_only`, survives redeploys). Not run here: Docker is not available to me, so the image build, the volume ownership and the compose boot are untested; the same code path is covered by `CursorProtectionSetupTests` (Production without path, a file where the directory should be, a writable directory, the development default). Keys are secrets: not committed, not in the SQL backup (say so in the runbook); a lost directory only invalidates outstanding cursors (400, restart from page 1). Concrete text in `docs/architecture/data-and-performance-rules.md` section 8.
- Also: the flaky opacity assertion in `PagingContractTests` now compares the plain base64url encoding, not the decimal id; `Limits.CursorMaxLength` comment states the real length (134 characters, limit 256); new `WarehouseScopeTests` case: a cursor taken from an unscoped (administrator) view does not widen what a warehouse-scoped user sees.
- Needs from be-architect / security-engineer: confirm the Production-requires-setting rule and that the key ring is not part of backups. Needs from the person who runs deployments: mount `DataProtection__KeysPath` on shared durable storage before a second instance or a rolling deploy.
- Open conflicts: none.
### 2026-10-12 be-framework -> be-dba, be-architect, integration-test-engineer, orchestrator (GUID PR-3: public id storage and plumbing)
- Produced (branch `feature/guid-pr3-public-id-storage`): `PublicEntity : Entity` (`src/Ims.Domain/Common/Models`, `PublicId` = `Guid.NewGuid()` set at construction, private setter, no method sets it) under the nine entities (User, Warehouse, Partner, Category, Uom, Product, ApprovalRule, StockDocument, Attachment); mapping in `AppDbContext.ConfigurePublicIds` (default `NEWID()` for raw inserts, unique index `UX_<Table>_PublicId`, FILLFACTOR 90, exempted from the soft-delete filter loop); constants in `PublicIdColumn`; migration `20261012031930_AddPublicIds` (5 restartable steps per table: add NULL column + default, batched backfill of 4,000 rows by clustered key, NOT NULL, rebuild of the clustered index, create the unique index; all `suppressTransaction`, Down drops index, default, column); Application: `ByPublicIdSpec<T>`, `PublicIdsSpec<T>`, `IIdMap<T>` / `IdMap<T>` (scoped, open generic, one query per list, per-direction memory, `IdPair`, `IdPairsSpec`); `SqlExceptionMapper` maps a duplicate public id to 409 `Messages.PublicIdInUse` ("try again"); `SchemaPin` has the nine `UX_*_PublicId` lines (unfiltered). Tests: `PublicIdMigrationTests` (Up/Down/Up with data, 9,500 partners across batches, restart after a half-done run), `PublicIdStorageTests`, `IdMapTests`, `IdMapRegistrationTests`, `Ims.Domain.Tests/Common/PublicEntityTests`.
- Migration measurements (1,000,000 documents + 100,000 attachments + 50,000 products, LocalDB ImsDev, warm; full text in `data-and-performance-rules.md` section 9): StockDocument add 0.02 s, backfill 11.7 s (250 batches), NOT NULL 2.5 s, clustered rebuild 4.0 s, unique index 1.7 s (27.8 MB, 90 % full); `dotnet ef database update` end to end 30 s (3 s is tool start-up), Down 3 s; log about 400 MB per million documents (a single `ADD ... NOT NULL DEFAULT NEWID()` is faster, 5 s against 14 s, and logs the same, but is one long lock). Without the rebuild the 16 extra bytes per row split every page: clustered index 558 MB at 51 % full, 98.6 % fragmented, so the rebuild is IN the migration. StockDocument with its twelve indexes grew about 47 MB (7 %). The backfill of a table is one command (about 86,000 rows/s): beyond about 5 million rows in one table it passes the application connection's 60 s command timeout (the Seeder migrates at start-up). That timeout is the constant `Limits.DbCommandTimeoutSeconds`: there is no setting, and `dotnet ef` cannot raise it either (it uses the same registration). Only the BACKFILL resumes after a timeout; NOT NULL, the rebuild and the index are single statements that roll back (they need about 24, 15 and 35 million rows to reach 60 s). For a bigger table run `dotnet ef migrations script` and `sqlcmd -t 0` in the window, or raise the constant for the window; the smallest permanent change would be an optional `Database:CommandTimeoutSeconds` setting in `AddPersistence` (not built).
- Needs from be-dba: review the migration; one decision to confirm: ONLINE index operations are not used (Express/LocalDB), so it is a maintenance-window migration. I changed ONE line in your `docs/dba/07-maintenance.sql`: indexes named `UX_*_PublicId` are rebuilt with FILLFACTOR 90 even on the tables the script rebuilds at 100 (StockDocument), otherwise the first rebuild would have silently undone the migration's fill factor. PR-10: measure fragmentation of the nine `UX_*_PublicId` indexes after the write mix.
- Needs from be-architect: DECISION TO CONFIRM, a deviation from ADR section 3.1 ("audit `KeyOf` uses `PublicId`"): in PR-3 `KeyOf` still returns the integer primary key, with a comment, because the audit page, its entity-id filter, the business events (`UserUpdated`, `Logout`, ... use `Id.ToString()`) and the API all still speak integers: switching only the row events would leave two ids for one user in one list, and nothing public can yet show the GUID. The `After`/`Before` payload of a created or deleted row now carries `PublicId` (tested), so the ids can be matched. Each flip PR (PR-4 to PR-7) switches `KeyOf` for its entities together with the business events and their tests. If you want `KeyOf` switched now, it is a one-line change plus about 6 audit assertions in `AuditTrailTests` / `TokenTests`.
- Decisions others must respect: the id is generated by the entity, never set from outside (EF reads it back through the private setter); the unique index is NOT filtered by `REC_DELETED`; `IdMap.ToInternalAsync` (client ids) hides soft-deleted rows, `ToPublicAsync` (ids of rows already read) names them; `IIdMap<T>` is per entity type, inject the one you need; the 9-table list is pinned three ways (`PublicEntityTests`, the model test, `SchemaPin`). Raw INSERT scripts (perf seed, tests) keep working because of the `NEWID()` default. `ROW_VERSION` of every row changes during the backfill (ETags held by open tabs go stale: maintenance window).
- Checklist for PR-4 (and PR-5 to PR-7 for their entities), also in the ADR step plan: switch `AppDbContext.KeyOf` to the `PublicId` for the flipped entities together with the business-event ids and their tests, and map the audit `EntityId`: after a flip the history mixes integers (old rows) and GUIDs (new rows), so the audit filter and page must cope with both, or the old rows are mapped.
- Not done on purpose (PR-4 to PR-7, PR-7b): no route, DTO, filter, JWT or audit change; no idempotent create; no `IHasPublicId` interface (the base class is the marker).
- Open conflicts: none. Run: `dotnet build Ims.sln -c Release -warnaserror --no-incremental` clean; Ims.Domain.Tests 740 passed, Ims.Architecture.Tests 68 passed, Ims.Api.Tests 1150 passed (+1 new test added afterwards and run filtered).

### 2026-10-12 be-framework -> fe-framework, be-architect, security-engineer, integration-test-engineer, behavior-test-engineer, orchestrator (GUID PR-5: user flip)
- Produced (branch `feature/guid-pr5-user-flip`, PR base `feature/public-ids-integration`, NOT main): a user is named by `User.PublicId` everywhere it crosses the wire. `UserInfo.id` (login, refresh, `GET /api/auth/me`) and `UserDto.id` are GUID strings; `PUT /api/users/{id:guid}` (the integer route is gone, 404; an unknown id is a 404 with the `UserNotFound` problem); `DocumentDto` and `DocumentListItem` `createdById`, `submittedById` and `returnedById` are GUIDs (projected from the joined `CreatedByUser` / `SubmittedByUser` / `RejectedByUser` row: no extra query, no look-up). Everything else that names a user was already text (`createdBy`, `approvedBy`, `postedBy`, `reversedBy`, `uploadedBy`, audit `actor` are user NAMES) or a name list, so it did not change; there is no user filter, user query parameter or user-id field in a request body besides the existing `warehouseIds` (already GUIDs since PR-4).
- JWT and security (the care point): the access token and `ICurrentUser.UserId` stay the integer; the GUID is not in the token (a test decodes the token and finds the integer claim and no GUID claim). `UserInfo.id` is the user's PublicId, so the web compares `userInfo.id === document.createdById` to know "mine". All ownership and approval rules compare integers: `DocumentActionPolicy` (staff/clerk own drafts, approver != creator, self-approval) gets its input from `[JsonIgnore] DocumentDto.CreatedByKey / SubmittedByKey` (same pattern as `WarehouseKey`; also on `DocumentListItem` for `canAct`) and `ICurrentUser.UserId`; the `mine` filter, drafts/approvals counts, `UpdateUserHandler` self-lock-out (`user.Id == current.UserId`) and last-admin (`ActiveAdminsSpec(user.Id)`) checks are unchanged integer comparisons. No request carries a user id except the admin-only `PUT /api/users/{id}`, so naming another user's GUID cannot impersonate anyone; a clerk who reads another clerk's `createdById` still gets 403 on edit/submit/cancel and no `edit`/`submit` in `allowedActions` (`UserFlipTests.Naming_another_users_public_id_gives_no_rights_over_their_documents`).
- Audit: `User` added to `AppDbContext.AuditedByPublicId`, together with the business events: `login.success/failed/locked/lockout`, `logout`, `user.created`, `user.updated`, `password.changed` now record `User.PublicId` as `EntityId` (`LogoutHandler` resolves it with one `IIdMap<User>.ToPublicAsync`, only on a real sign-out). Old rows keep the integer (as in PR-4; tested with a legacy row); the audit `actor` stays the user name.
- Decisions others must respect: (1) `UserInfo.id` is the PublicId, NOT the integer: the web must not use it as a number or put it in a URL other than `PUT /api/users/{id}`. (2) `DocumentDto`/`DocumentListItem` constructors gained two trailing `[JsonIgnore]` integers; anything building them by hand must pass them. (3) `UserWithWarehousesByPublicIdSpec` (Users feature) is the by-public-id look-up for the update; `Auth.UserWithWarehousesSpec(int)` stays for the session (`current.UserId`).
- Needs from fe-framework (PR-8): regenerate the client; `UserInfo.id`, `UserDto.id`, `DocumentDto/DocumentListItem.createdById/submittedById/returnedById` become `string` (uuid, the last two nullable); `PUT /api/users/{id}` takes the GUID; stores that compare the signed-in user's id with a document's `createdById` keep working with strings (`===`); R06 preferences keyed `ims.pref.<userId>.*` get the GUID string (old integer-keyed preferences are simply not found: reset, harmless); R18 recents keyed by user; drop any `Number(...)`/numeric typing of the user id. Product, document and attachment ids are still integers until PR-6/PR-7.
- Needs from behavior-test-engineer: `tests/smoke/smoke.mjs` line 259 asserted `createdById > 0`; I changed it to "a 36-character string" (one line). `e2e/src/api.ts` posts `/api/users` (response id typed as number; values come from the API, types only).
- Tests: `Tests/PublicIds/UserFlipTests` (5: ids stored = ids on the wire for create/list/login/me/refresh and a string on the wire; token keeps the integer and no GUID claim; user route GUID only, integer route 404, unknown GUID 404; another user's GUID gives no rights; audit events and rows by PublicId plus a legacy integer row); `OpenApiDocumentTests` "no integer id on the wire" extended to `UserDto`, `UserInfo`, `createdById/submittedById/returnedById` and `PUT /api/users/{id}`; builders, `TestAccount.Id`, `UsersClient`, `EndpointCatalog` use Guid. Not added: a last-admin test (the shared test database always holds other active admins, so the protection cannot be reached without disabling them; the code path is unchanged and still integer; self-lock-out is covered by `SessionSecurityTests`). Docs: `backend.md` (Public ids), `data-and-performance-rules.md` section 9.

Table or cards is decided by the table's own box (`container: ims-table / inline-size`, 45rem = `width('table-wide')`; a 1024px window gives 752px, so 768 and below are still cards). ### 2026-10-13 be-framework -> security-engineer, fe-framework, be-architect, orchestrator (PID-01 and PID-02: no counters on the wire)
- Produced (branch `fix/pid-01-02-counters`, PR base `feature/public-ids-integration`): PID-01: `LedgerRow` and `AuditLogDto` have no `id` in the JSON (the internal id is a `[JsonIgnore] Key`, still the seek position of the encrypted cursor); the OpenAPI integer-id allow-list is empty (only the text `AuditLogDto.entityId` stays). PID-02: `concurrencyToken` and the ETag are now `IDocumentVersion.Token(publicId, rowVersion)` = base64url of the first 16 bytes of HMAC-SHA256 (key = HKDF of the JWT signing key, context `Ims.DocVersion.v1`); `EnsureCurrent` recomputes and compares in constant time; anything else (old 8-byte form, forged, other document, any length) is the same 409 as a stale token. Client regenerated (base URL kept); the audit "Show" button label uses the row's place in the list (`entry {number}`) instead of the id.
- Decision others must respect: the token is NOT the Data Protection AEAD the task suggested. A randomized AEAD token gives a new string on every read, and the web compares the list item's token with the opened document's token (`$isStale`, `currentToken`), so it would always show "changed by someone else". A keyed hash is deterministic, equally uncountable and needs no new secret. Consequence: rotating the JWT signing key gives open editors one 409. Only documents expose a rowversion-like value; no other DTO does.
- Needs from be-architect: one line in `backend.md` (documents: the token is a keyed hash, `DocumentVersion`). Needs from security-engineer: rerun `counters.mjs` and `enum.mjs`. Web: a spec or helper must not read `id` of a ledger or audit row, or parse the token.
- Review round (PR 80 security review, 3 minors): `backend.md` now says the token is a keyed hash (done); a test asserts the exact JSON member set of a ledger row and an audit row; `If-Match` that names no version (`""`, `W/""`, only commas) is now 428 instead of skipping the check like `*` (pre-existing gap in `DocumentsController.IfMatch`), with tests.
- Open conflicts: none.

### 2026-10-16 be-framework -> fe-framework, be-architect, project-manager, orchestrator (B21 + B22: recall, cancel on Approved)
- Produced (branch `feature/doc-corrections-b21-b22`, one PR; send back is NOT built, lean-scope 6.3): `POST /api/documents/{id}/recall` (op `Documents_recall`, no body, `DocumentCreate`) and `POST .../cancel` now also cancels an Approved document. Domain: `StockDocument.Recall()` (Submitted -> Draft, clears submit fields and flags, no Returned badge, number and lines kept), `Cancel` takes Approved with a mandatory trimmed reason of 5+ characters (`ApprovedBy/At` stay as history), `DocumentActionPolicy.MayRecall` (the submitter, create right) and `MayReviewApproved` (the reviewer the document is routed to: Supervisor not Large, Owner Large only, Admin any; the submitter is not excluded). `allowedActions`: `recall` for the submitter of a Submitted document; `cancel` on Approved for those reviewers. Audit `document.recalled` (before Status + RequiresApproval, after Status); cancel keeps `document.cancelled` (before Status Approved). No migration, no new column. New door policy `AuthPolicies.DocumentCancel` = create OR approve right (the Owner reaches the handler, which still refuses a Draft or Submitted cancel with 403).
- Behaviour to respect: recall of a Draft that is not Returned is an idempotent 200 with no write and no audit, checked BEFORE the token (a retry with the old token is not an error); a Submitted document not submitted by the caller is 403 (`OwnRecallOnly`), also for Supervisor and Admin; any other state is the usual 400 `Messages.OnlySubmittedCanRecall` (the design's "wrong state"), and the real race (approve or post won) is 409 `document_changed` because the token moved. Cancel Approved: wrong role 403 `OnlyReviewerCanCancelApproved` comes BEFORE the reason check; short or missing reason is 400 (`CancelApprovedReasonRequired`, `ReasonTooShort`); Draft and Submitted cancel still need no reason. `Messages.OnlyDraftOrSubmittedCanCancel` was renamed `OnlyDraftSubmittedOrApprovedCanCancel` with the new text. Attachments: stay through a recall (Draft again: removable); on Cancelled they stay readable and take no new file (existing rule). Nothing is posted by either transition, so no balance lock is needed; the row version decides races (tests: recall vs post, recall vs approve, cancel vs post: exactly one winner).

### 2026-10-16 be-framework -> orchestrator, integration-test-engineer (fix: soft deletes of audited rows wrote no 'deleted' audit row)
- Root cause (`AppDbContext`): `StampAuditColumns` kept the soft-deleted entries in a `HashSet<EntityEntry>` and `CollectAudit` looked them up with `softDeleted.Contains(e)` on entries from a second `ChangeTracker.Entries()` call; `EntityEntry` objects are made per call and compare by reference, so it never matched. The row then looked like a plain update whose only changed property (REC_DELETED) is on the skip list, so no row at all. The same hole hid `MarkDeleted()` (state Modified from the start).
- Fix: `IsSoftDelete(entry)` = Modified and REC_DELETED original false, current true; the set and the return value are gone. A delete combined with other edits gives one `deleted` row with the values from before. Same save and transaction as before; no new query. Audited types that now audit deletes: Product, ProductBarcode, UomConversion, Warehouse, Partner, Category, Uom, ApprovalRule, User. Today only barcodes and conversions removed from a product are deleted through a real use case; no endpoint deletes the others, so their rows are written by `Remove`/`MarkDeleted` only (tests go through the DbContext).
- Tests: `Audit/RowDeletionAuditTests` (7 types x Remove and MarkDeleted, change+delete, plain update guard, barcode and conversion removal), `Audit/DeletedRowsOnTheAuditPageTests` (page by public id, action filter, 403 for non-Owner/Admin). `PublicIdDatabase.NewContext` takes an optional user and clock; `Support/FixedUser`.
- Measured SaveChanges of 5,000 inserted audited products (same machine, 5 runs, first is a cold run): before 1.6-2.2 s steady, after 1.3-2.2 s; update of 5,000: 1.6 s before, 1.5-1.6 s after. No difference beyond noise.
- Decisions others must respect: do not keep `EntityEntry` objects across `Entries()` calls; read the state from the entry. Open conflicts: none.

### 2026-10-16 fe-framework -> orchestrator, unit-test-engineer (fix: flaky `single-announcement.spec.ts`, R11)
- Produced (branch `fix/flaky-single-announcement`): `web/libs/core/http/src/lib/interceptors/single-announcement.spec.ts` only. No production change.
- Cause: the test slept a fixed 50 ms for the async error-body read (`readErrorBody`: `Blob.text()` or the jsdom `FileReader` chain, several macrotasks) before the interceptor shows the toast and the screen sets its banner. On a loaded runner the 50 ms timer fires before that chain finishes, so the live regions are still empty (`[]`). Reproduced on demand by delaying the body read by 150 ms: all 3 tests failed with `Received: []`, the exact CI symptom.
- Fix: the screen's `run()` returns a promise that resolves in the call's error callback (after the interceptor's `tap` showed the toast and the screen set its banner); `fail()` awaits it, then `detectChanges()`. Assertions unchanged (exactly one announcement; control still expects two). The same run with the 150 ms delay now passes.
- Production code has no race: the announcement is a signal write rendered by the next `detectChanges`; the only deferred work is `afterNextRender` for scrolling, which does not affect what is announced.
- Scanned and left as is: `error.interceptor.spec.ts` (awaits the failing call's own result), `toast-host`/`toast.service` specs (fake timers, synchronous), home/login/document-editor alert specs (mocked operations, synchronous). Not touched, same class but not live-region: fixed sleeps for debounce in `audit-log.component.spec.ts` (400 ms, twice) and `document-list.component.spec.ts` (`SEARCH_DEBOUNCE_MS + 50`); candidates for fake timers if they ever flake.
- Decisions others must respect: a spec that waits for an HTTP error handled by `errorInterceptor` awaits the call's own error callback, never a timer.
- Open conflicts: none.
- Also fixed (test only): e2e `17-feedback` "sign-in 500 with a sentence" expected the API's sentence, but the sign-in page (R25) maps a 500 to its own translated line (login spec LG-18, `login.error.server`; `login-failure.ts` is right). The test now expects that line once, no API sentence, no toast. Not run (needs the API and a throw-away DB).
- Evidence: 20 of 20 runs of `single-announcement.spec.ts` pass with 24 busy loops on 12 cores (run time up to 264 s).

### 2026-10-17 be-framework -> orchestrator, be-architect, unit-test-engineer, fe-framework (RP-3 replacement: claims in the token, replaces closed PR #99)
- Produced (branch `feature/roles-rp3-claims-in-token`): the access claims go in the token, per the owner decision; nothing is looked up per request. `IAccessSnapshotLoader` / `AccessSnapshotLoader` (uncached, kept from #99; an inactive role also gives no snapshot), `TokenService` writes `role` (display and audit), one `perm` per permission, `allwh`, `wh` only when `allwh` is false, `acv` = `ClaimNames.CurrentClaimsVersion` (1; emitted, **not checked**: the `OnTokenValidated` check ships with RP-4a). Access token 15 -> 5 minutes (`Limits.AccessTokenMinutes`, `Jwt:AccessMinutes`). Login and refresh load the snapshot before any write: a missing or inactive role is a 401 with no new refresh row, no rotation and no success audit. `UserInfo` gains `roleName` and `seesAllWarehouses`; sign-in and refresh build it from the snapshot, `/me` from the presented token's claims. `UpdateUserHandler` revokes refresh tokens only on deactivation or a password change. ADR decision 6, section 3 and the Freshness row rewritten; `backend.md` token statement fixed. Discarded from #99: the cache, `IAccessSnapshots` (Forget), the bearer event, `HttpItemKeys`, `AccessOptions`, `CurrentUser` reading `HttpContext.Items`.
- Staleness, stated plainly: rights are as old as the token. A demotion or role edit applies at the next refresh, so a demoted person keeps the old rights for up to about 5.5 minutes (5 minutes plus the 30 s clock skew); a demoted administrator can still administer for that window, and the last-administrator guard reads the database so they cannot lock the system out with it. A deactivated or password-changed user cannot renew (refresh tokens revoked, sign-in refused) but the token in hand keeps working for up to about 5.5 minutes. Logout revokes the refresh token only; the access token it leaves runs out on its own. SEC-06 and ER-15 are narrowed from 15 to about 5.5 minutes, not closed.
- Needs from fe-framework / ui-ux: the role editor (RP-5) should say "Changes apply within 5 minutes". The web needs no change here (`UserInfo` fields are additive; it does not read the token lifetime, and the interceptor already refreshes on 401). The generated api-client / OpenAPI copy in `web/` was not regenerated; regenerate it when the web next touches the auth types.
- Decisions others must respect: the endpoint policies and `CurrentUser.Role` still decide by the `role` claim and the fixed map in this slice (enforcement unchanged), so the `perm` claims are written and tested but not yet enforced; RP-4a switches the policies to them and adds the `acv` check. `UserInfo.permissions` is still the fixed 15-code list. Each page keeps its own columns through the mixin argument (users `2fr 1fr`, products `2fr 1fr 1fr`, partners `2fr 1fr auto`, stock 12rem auto-fit, the rest 11rem auto-fit). `app-form-field` and `app-date-field` are untouched.
- Measured (live app, admin, light and dark, en, zh, ms): search box and checkbox level with the other boxes (top, bottom, centre spread 0 px) on stock, products, partners, users, audit, receipts, issues, reports at 360, 768, 1280; with a reversed range no box moves (0 px, was up to 25.75 px at 1480 and 69.25 px at 1024) at 1480, 1024, 768, 640, 600, 360 and the error stays inside the page (no sideways scroll). No label wrapped in a filter row at any width tested, so no label `min-height`.
- Decisions others must respect: a new filter row uses `@include filter-row` (the columns as the argument), never its own grid; a control without a label placed in it needs the same top margin (add it to the mixin). A grid row that wraps still moves the rows below it when the To error appears (nothing can avoid that); only the boxes of the row holding To are fixed.
- Known limit: text-only zoom 200% at 768 makes the `2fr 1fr auto` columns of Partners and Products too narrow (the Type label wraps to 3 lines); not touched here (columns were already so), browser zoom is fine.

### 2026-10-17 be-framework -> security-engineer, integration-test-engineer, orchestrator
- Produced (branch `fix/refresh-rate-limit`): the sign-in/refresh rate-limit fix from the security reviews of #102 and #104. Backend: refresh gets its own `RateLimitPolicies.Refresh` (300 per minute per address, `Limits.RefreshRateLimitPerMinute`); `login` stays 20 per minute. Policies are separate limiters, so neither bucket can use up the other. `Retry-After` and `retryAfterSeconds` on 429 are unchanged. Web: `AuthService` no longer ends the session on any refresh failure; only 401 or 403 does. 429, 5xx and network errors are retried twice (server `retryAfterSeconds` capped at 10 s, else 1 s then 2 s) and then leave the user signed in (the next 401 refreshes again). App-start `restoreSession` never waits or retries. Constants in `identity.const.ts` (`REFRESH_RETRY`).
- Tests: `tests/Ims.Api.Tests/Tests/Auth/RefreshRateLimitTests.cs` (35 refreshes from one address not limited; login limited at 20 while refresh from the same address still works; refresh ceiling at 300 with `Retry-After`); `auth.service.spec.ts` (429 with Retry-After then success, cap, backoff and stay signed in, 403 ends the session, app start does not wait); `auth.interceptor.spec.ts` (next 401 refreshes again after a failed refresh). Docs: limiter table in `docs/security/refresh-cookie.md`, `dast.md` row.
- Needs from security-engineer: full review (auth-adjacent). Questions to look at: is 300 per address right for the biggest office (a per-token partition was considered and dropped: a client choosing its own tokens gets a new bucket each time, so the address stays the key); the 403 from a failed CSRF check ends the session, as before.
- Decisions others must respect: login limit stays 20 and keeps its own bucket; refresh never shares a bucket with login; a refresh that failed for any reason but 401/403 must not sign the user out. PR #104's `acv` check relies on this (every user refreshes once at deploy).
- Open conflicts: none.

### 2026-10-19 fe-framework -> orchestrator, fe-architect, security-engineer, qa-edge-accessibility, behavior-test-engineer (PR #135: the service worker caches no API data; session expiry keeps the document draft)
- Produced (branch `fix/sw-cache-and-session-expiry`): (1) S1 and defect 2: `web/apps/main/ngsw-config.json` has no `dataGroups`, so the worker never caches or answers an API call (root cause: the worker keeps a handle on the `master-data` Cache and went on answering after `caches.delete`, so the clearing in `PwaService` never worked); `PwaService` still removes `:data:` caches at start and at session end, best effort; `ngsw-config.spec.ts` pins the config. (2) Defect 1: `AuthService` emits `sessionExpiring$` before the user is cleared and exposes `$sessionExpired`; the sign-in notice says plainly whether changes are kept or lost (`reason=expired-kept` or `expired`; en, zh, ms) and is tied to the username field by `aria-describedby` (new `extraDescription` input of `app-form-field`); `unsavedChangesGuard` stands aside once the session expired; `SessionDraftService` keeps the document editor's form in sessionStorage as `{ savedAt, value }` (per user id, 15 minutes, 1,000,000 characters at most, removed at explicit sign-out and when another user signs in); the editor restores it once after sign-in through `parseEditorDraft` (version 1, shape, at most 500 lines, field lengths, ids through `normalizePublicId`; a draft that fails, or was made on another version of the document, is dropped with the "not restored" notice). The editor now resets the root-provided `DocumentsStore.detail` before loading a saved document, so the form is filled once (a stale copy used to overwrite the restored draft after an in-app sign-in). Review fixes M1 to M4: release note and the `safety-worker.js` kill switch in frontend.md decision 18, TTL and validation, `serve-dist.mjs` answers 400 to `GET /%`. e2e: `35-service-worker` (production build; fails on the old config), and in `08-session` a new receipt and a saved draft that expire during Save and come back after an in-app sign-in. `serve-dist.mjs` takes `API_URL` (the worker bypasses Playwright routes); `e2e.yml` and the README pass it.
- Decisions others must respect: docs/architecture/frontend.md decisions 18 and 19. No API data in the worker (cost: no offline pickers or lists, no 12 h repeat-visit speed-up; stated in decision 18); offline reads need a network-first per-user design and a security review. Only the document editor keeps unsent work at expiry; the product, partner, user and role forms do not (TASKS.md follow-up).
- Open conflicts: **R26** (`docs/design/2026-refresh-plan.md`, re-sign-in in place) bans sessionStorage for the editor draft. The coordinator decided that this PR's stored draft is accepted for now (security review approved it with the TTL, validation and per-user clearing); a SUPERSEDE line is in the R26 acceptance, and R26 stays the preferred long-term fix and will remove the need for the stored draft.
- Needs from security-engineer: re-test S1 on a production build, and the review minors M2 (a bad value under the draft key), M3 (`savedAt`, wait past the TTL) and M4 (`GET /%`). Needs from fe-architect: review of decisions 18 and 19. Not fixed here (other lanes or other defects): S2 and S3 (API), defects 3 and 10 (API), 4 to 9.

(2) `tests/Ims.Api.Tests/Tests/Documents/PostingStatementCountTests.cs` (+ `Support/SqlStatementLog.cs`): SQL commands and insert/update/delete statements per operation (submit, post, read, reverse; receipt and issue; one and three products) of an untracked product through the real host, frozen as `(commands, writes)`; the post of a one-product receipt is 8 commands and 5 writes.

### 2026-10-19 be-framework -> orchestrator, security-engineer, be-architect, be-system-designer, integration-test-engineer, fe-framework (PL1.1: module foundation, backend)
- Produced (branch `feature/pl11-module-foundation`, review tier FULL): Domain `Modules/` (`ModuleCodes`, `ModuleDefinition`, `ModuleCatalog` with the one entry `accounting`; `SettingKeys.ForModule`), permission `modules.manage` (Administration, dangerous, not AdminCore, Admin only through `PermissionCatalog.Codes`), Application `Features/Modules/` (`IModuleSwitches` + `ModuleSwitches` reading its own `Module.*` rows once per request, `ListModules`, `SwitchModule` with validator and handler), `ITransactionLocker.LockModulesAsync` (resource `modules`, `retry: false`), `UserInfo.Modules` built in `UserInfoMapper` (sign-in, refresh, `/me`), Api `ModulesController`, `[RequiresModule]` and `ModuleGateMiddleware` (wired between `UseAuthentication` and `UseAuthorization`), migration `20261019133035_GrantModulesManage` (data only). Tests: Domain `ModuleCatalogTests`; Architecture `ModuleGateTests`; Api `Tests/Modules/*` (list, flip, audit, 400/404/403/401, forged tokens, `UserInfo.modules`, race of 8 flips, lock 409, gate matrix on a probe controller), `GrantModulesManageMigrationTests`.
- Contract change (for fe-framework / CS6, client NOT regenerated here): new `GET /api/modules` (any signed-in user, operationId `Modules_list`, returns `ModuleDto[]` = `code, name, description, enabled, requires[]`) and `PUT /api/modules/{code}` (`modules.manage`, `Modules_switch`, body `ModuleSwitchRequest` = `enabled` (required, boolean) and `reason` (required, at least 5 characters), returns `ModuleDto`; 400 short reason or missing `enabled`, 404 unknown code, 409 busy lock); `UserInfo` gains the required property `modules: string[]` (codes that are on, catalog order). Checked by diffing the new OpenAPI document against `web/openapi/openapi.raw.json`: only these additions, no existing path or schema changed.
- Migration: ONE, `GrantModulesManage`, data only (one `RolePermission` row for the Admin role; no schema change, snapshot unchanged). `Down` deletes every row of the code `modules.manage`, in any role. Baton: `gh pr list` showed no other open PR with a migration; PL1.2 (`GrantAccountingExport`) may add its migration only after this one merges. Deploy note: an Administrator gets `modules.manage` at the next token refresh (up to 5 minutes), or by signing in again.
- Decisions others must respect: (1) a module with no `Module.<code>` row, a code not in the catalog, or a value other than exactly `true` is off; the core has no switch. (2) The gate answers an EMPTY 404, so the body is the problem details of an unknown route (acceptance 5); the web cannot tell "turned off" from "no such route" by the body: on a 404 from a module endpoint it should refetch `GET /api/modules` (PL1.4). (3) A flip to the state a module already has is a 200 with no write and no audit event. (4) The audit event is `module.enabled` / `module.disabled`, entity `SystemSetting`, entity id `Module.<code>`, before `{Module, Enabled}`, after `{Module, Enabled, Reason}` (reason trimmed). (5) `Enabled` is `bool?` in the request so a body without it is a 400 and never a quiet switch off. (6) `Requires` is in the contract (always empty) but not enforced yet; `CanDisable` is not built (plan: PL2). (7) A busy `modules` lock is the existing 409 Conflict problem (title "Conflict") with the sentence `Messages.ModulesLockBusy`; there is no `code` extension. (8) Posting and reversal code never reads a switch (architecture test `ThePostingCode_NeverReadsAModuleSwitch`). (9) A new permission needs the independent table `RoleMatrix` (tests) and `RolesMigrationTests.AddedLater` updated; both were.
- Needs from security-engineer: probe the gate as every role with a module off and on (the test host uses a probe controller; the real controllers arrive in PL1.6). Needs from be-architect / be-system-designer: confirm the lock placement (read, write and audit under `modules`) and the decision to keep `ModuleSwitches` reading its own rows instead of sharing the `DbSystemSettings` read (plan risk 7 fallback; one tiny query only on sign-in, `/me` and module requests). Needs from fe-framework: contract freeze CS6 after PL1.2 merges (not before).
- Open conflicts: none. Deviations from the plan: `ModuleDto` also carries `description` (one sentence for the Modules screen); the plan listed `{code, name, enabled, requires}`. Hand-written changes are about 1,300 lines (source 351, tests 949; the plan said 900, the difference is tests), plus the 1,925 generated lines of the migration `Designer.cs`.

### 2026-10-20 be-framework -> orchestrator, security-engineer, be-architect, integration-test-engineer, fe-framework (PL1.2: module-aware roles + accounting.export)
- Produced (branch `feature/pl12-module-aware-roles`, review tier FULL): Domain `PermissionDefinition.Module`, the 23rd permission `accounting.export` (group Stock and reports, `Requires` `cost.view`, module `accounting`), `ModulePermissions` (`IsOffered`, `NotOffered`, `Offered`, `Merge`), `SystemRoleDefaults` (Admin through the catalog, Owner added), message `PermissionNotAvailable`. Application: `ListPermissionsHandler` and `RoleReader` omit the permissions of an off module (so `GET /api/roles/{id}` never lists a hidden grant); `ReplaceRolePermissionsHandler` refuses an off-module code (400) and merges the role's hidden grants back (never in `added`/`removed`/audit; `Requires` closure on the merged set, checked after the aggregate so the Administrator lock still answers 409 first); `CreateRoleHandler` refuses an off-module code in a list and copies only what the source shows; `SwitchModuleHandler` now counts the live roles holding a permission of the module when it is turned on and returns `ModuleSwitchResult` (module fields plus `rolesRegainingGrants`) and writes `RolesRegainingGrants` into the `module.enabled` audit event. Migration `GrantAccountingExport` (data only).
- Contract change (client NOT regenerated, for PL1.3 / CS6): `PermissionDto` gains `module` (string, nullable); `PUT /api/modules/{code}` answers the new schema `ModuleSwitchResult` (`code, name, description, enabled, requires[], rolesRegainingGrants`) instead of `ModuleDto` (same fields plus the count; `GET /api/modules` stays `ModuleDto[]`); `GET /api/permissions` and every role read omit permissions of an off module; new 400 text "'{code}' is not available while its module is off." on `POST /api/roles` and `PUT /api/roles/{id}/permissions`. `UserInfo` unchanged.
- Migration: ONE, `GrantAccountingExport` (20261019145353), data only, snapshot unchanged: a row for the Admin and Owner system roles with `NOT EXISTS` guards; `Down` deletes every row of the code `accounting.export`. `gh pr list` showed no other open PR with a migration (PL1.1 #146 is merged). Baton: PL1.5 (`AddTaxReadyFields`) may add its migration after this merges. Deploy note: an Administrator or Owner gets `accounting.export` at the next token refresh (up to 5 minutes) or by signing in again; a custom role that holds all 22 older permissions no longer covers the Administrator or Owner role (they now hold a 23rd), so its holders get 403 when editing those roles until it is given `accounting.export` in the role editor (it can be given only while the accounting module is on).
- Decisions others must respect: (1) `accounting.export` is NOT dangerous (the ADR does not flag it; it reads, changes nothing, is audited from PL1.6, and needs `cost.view`, which is dangerous); pinned by `PermissionCatalogTests`, flagging later is a one-line diff for security-engineer to decide. (2) `GET /api/roles/{id}` omits hidden grants (the plan's default). (3) `UserInfo.permissions` and the token claims are NOT filtered by the switch (ADR 4.3, acceptance 7): the web shows an item by module AND permission, and a filtered list would go stale after a flip until the next refresh. (4) A role copy takes what the source shows, so a hidden grant is not copied unseen. (5) Containment counts hidden grants (unchanged): while a module is off, a custom role that holds every OFFERED permission does not cover a role that holds a hidden one; tests that need "every permission" turn the module on first. (6) PL1.2 touches the switch handler only for the regained-grants count (plan acceptance 5), nothing else in it changed.
- Tests: Domain 936 + new `ModulePermissionsTests`; API: `ModuleAwareRolesTests` (11, real SQL Server), 2 probe/gate tests (claim present while off, 404 off / 403 or 200 on, forged tokens), `GrantAccountingExportMigrationTests` (5: fresh = upgraded, idempotent, Down/Up with a custom holder, DBA script), catalog count 23, pinned dangerous list unchanged, RoleMatrix and the AddedLater lists of the two older migration tests updated. ### 2026-10-20 fe-framework -> orchestrator, be-framework, security-engineer (PR #147 review follow-up)
- Produced (same branch, one follow-up commit after the review APPROVE-WITH-MINORS): (1) a 401 is called a lockout only when the API sent a sentence with it; one without (a refresh that failed on the way) gets the generic sentence in a warning banner. (2) Every 400 without field errors is the wrong current password and shows our translated sentence; the code no longer matches the API's English text. (4) A visually hidden, read-only username input (`autocomplete="username"`, `aria-hidden`, `tabindex="-1"`, filled from the signed-in user) sits in the form for password managers; specs and e2e 38 assert it exists, is not announced and holds no password.
- Decision for be-framework / security-engineer (not changed): `/api/auth/change-password` was NOT added to `AUTH_ENDPOINTS`. That list is also what keeps the bearer token off a request (login, refresh, logout), and this call needs the token, so adding it would make every change a 401. Keeping the bearer but skipping the refresh-and-replay needs a second list in `auth.interceptor.ts` and changes what an expired access token does on this call (it would fail instead of refreshing); so a lockout 401 still causes one refresh and one more send of the two passwords (a second failed-login count). Decide whether to split the list; the API could also answer a lockout with a code that is not 401.
- Added to TASKS.md (not fixed here): a second open tab keeps its session for up to 5 minutes (no BroadcastChannel, same as logout); `POST change-password` has no `EnableRateLimiting`; `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH` sits in `pagination.const.ts` (wrong file name for all the limits in it, left alone).
- Open conflicts: none.

### 2026-10-20 be-framework -> orchestrator, be-dba, security-engineer, be-architect, fe-framework (PL1.5: tax-ready fields)
- Produced (branch `feature/pl15-tax-fields`, review tier FULL): migration `AddTaxReadyFields` (20261020022457): nullable `Product.HsCode` nvarchar(16), `Product.SstCode` nvarchar(20), `Partner.Tin` nvarchar(20), `Partner.SstRegNo` nvarchar(30); no default, no index, no CHECK. Metadata-only add (proved by a log-record count on 25,000 rows plus a check of the operations and the generated script). `Down` refuses (THROW 50004) while any row, live or soft-deleted, holds a value; the check and the drops are ONE batch so a script run by hand that goes on after an error drops nothing. Domain: `TaxFieldShapes` (one definition of the shape: HS code digits and dots; SST code and SST reg. no. letters, digits, hyphen; TIN letters and digits; ASCII only; trim only, no case change, blank = none), `Product.SetTaxCodes`, `Partner.SetTaxIdentifiers` (not part of `Update`, so a save that does not carry them cannot wipe them). Application: `ModuleFields.AcceptAsync` (off + non-blank value = 400 naming the field, off + nothing = leave stored values; on = replace, null clears) and `ShapeAsync` helpers (a read leaves the fields out while off; the module is asked only when a value is there to show, so reads of rows without any cost no extra query). Import: products template and file take `HsCode` and `SstCode` only while `accounting` is on; off, a file carrying either column is refused as a whole (400); on, a present column replaces that field for every row (a blank cell clears, like Category) and an absent column leaves stored values alone.
- Contract change (client REGENERATED in this PR, CS7 folded in by the orchestrator's decision because the PL1.3 drift test fails any contract change without it: `npm run api:generate` against this branch, `baseUrl` literals back to 5277, `servers` entry back to 5109; additions only, optional properties, no spec fallout): `ProductSaveRequest` gains optional `hsCode`, `sstCode`; `PartnerSaveRequest` gains optional `tin`, `sstRegNo` (all default null); `ProductDto` gains `hsCode?`, `sstCode?`; `PartnerDto` gains `tin?`, `sstRegNo?` (string, `[JsonIgnore(WhenWritingNull)]`: absent when the module is off or the value is empty, not `null`). New 400 text "'{field}' is not available while the accounting module is off." with `errors[field]`, and shape texts per field. `GET /api/import/templates/products` has two more columns while the module is on. No new endpoint, no new permission.
- Decisions others must respect: (1) trim only, case kept as typed (the plan names no normalisation); (2) blank counts as "no value" everywhere, including when the module is off; (3) no message and no log line contains the value (a TIN can be a person's); audit stays admin-only and shows old/new values through the existing row audit (verified by test); (4) partner TIN is returned only on `GET /api/partners` (`masterdata.read`) and the two write replies (policy unchanged); (5) no index: nothing searches or joins on the four columns (PL1.6 reads them while exporting, a join to Product/Partner already on the key); (6) architecture test `TaxFieldContractTests` pins which contracts may carry the fields and that responses omit them when null.
- Needs from be-dba: review the migration (Up metadata-only, Down guard); confirm sizes and the allowed characters against the accountant's package before PL1.6. Needs from security-engineer: the Full-tier probe (TIN not in logs/errors/audit scrub, off module cannot write or read the fields). Needs from fe-framework: CS7 (regeneration) is folded into this PR by the orchestrator's instruction once #149 is merged; PL1.8 consumes it. Spreadsheet: a General-format cell holds a number, so 0901.21 would arrive as 901.21 and 8471.30 as 8471.3; the import therefore REFUSES a number cell in the HsCode or SstCode column with a row error ("Type it as text"; no value echoed; a custom-formatted number cell is refused too). PL1.8's import hint must say to format the column as Text first. Not run: a manual start against `Ims_Dev` (the integration tests cover the same paths on a throw-away database; not applying a migration to the shared dev database).

- Decisions others must respect: (1) Quantity and Unit are per BASE unit of the product, Unit cost is per base unit (4 decimals, never signed), Amount is the stored line amount (2 decimals); Quantity and Amount are signed by `StockDirection` (receipt +, issue -, reversal the opposite), a zero is a plain 0. (2) A reversal row is dated by the REVERSAL (Kuala Lumpur day), like the movements report, so each period adds up to its own change; its `Document no.` and `Reversal of` both hold the original document number. (3) Over the cap: 400 "Too many rows: one accounting file holds at most 50000 rows. Narrow the date range (for example one month at a time) or pick one warehouse.", asked by reading 50,001 rows, no file, no audit. (4) A warehouse id that does not exist or is outside the caller's scope is the same 404 "Warehouse not found."; without a warehouse id the caller's scope still applies. (5) The handler refuses with 403 when the caller lacks `cost.view` even if the policy let the request in (the closure normally makes that impossible). (6) Text from people goes through `SpreadsheetText.Safe` (a value starting with = + - @ tab CR gets a leading quote; ClosedXML turns that into Excel's quote-prefix flag, so the stored text is UNCHANGED and the cell is text and stays text when edited; a test pins this in the file). Not applied to the four older exports (they store text cells explicitly, so nothing runs when opened, but the quote-prefix flag is missing): a one-line follow-up if security-engineer wants it. (7) One audit event per file produced, written before the answer: actor, time, file name as entity id, `{From, To, Warehouse (public id as asked), LimitedToWarehouses, Rows}`; never rows, names, SKUs, TIN or amounts. A refused request writes nothing. (8) `ClosedXmlExcelWriter` (shared by all exports) now sets number formats per column and fits widths to the first 2,000 rows (a sheet up to 2,000 rows is exactly as before): 50,000 x 17 cells went from about 27 s (first call, before) to about 8 s (second call, after) in a probe, and 13 to 21 s for the first call after; this is the only change outside the module.

Shown while `accounting` is on (`ModulesStore.isEnabled`) and then ALWAYS sent: both keys of a pair on every save, a loaded value goes back as loaded, an emptied field goes as `""` (the server's `TaxFieldShapes.Clean` stores a blank as none; `null` would be the same on the wire), nothing is sent while the module is off (an `undefined` key is not serialised). Shape is checked on the TRIMMED value, nothing is cut while typing (no `maxlength`), the value sent is the trimmed one, case as typed (`TAX_FIELD` in `@ims/models`, `taxFieldShape`/`cleanTaxField` in `@ims/utils`; a spec reads `Limits.cs` and `TaxFieldShapes.cs` and fails when the lengths or characters drift).   - Stale-module guard (a decision beyond the brief): a record that CARRIES a tax value proves the server had the module on when it answered (it leaves the values out while off), so a form whose module list is older than the record (another tab or an Admin switched the module on; the list refreshes with the token, about 5 minutes) still shows and sends the values instead of clearing them. Without it the old-list screen would send no key and the server (module on) would clear the stored value. Specs cover it for both forms.

### 2026-10-20 be-framework -> orchestrator, fe-framework, integration-test-engineer, security-engineer, project-manager (PL1.8 API follow-up: presence of the four tax keys)
- Produced (branch `feature/api-tax-field-presence`, **Full** tier: contract-adjacent, data-loss class): `Optional<T>` + `OptionalJsonConverter` (`src/Ims.Application/Common`), the four request properties `ProductSaveRequest.HsCode/SstCode` and `PartnerSaveRequest.Tin/SstRegNo` now `Optional<string>` (init properties outside the JSON constructor, `WhenWritingDefault`), `ProductWriter`, `CreatePartnerHandler`, `UpdatePartnerHandler` (`request.X.Or(entity.X)`), validators on `.Value` (`HasTaxFieldShape(property, error)`), `OptionalSchemaTransformer` (OpenAPI), docs (ADR 4.3 "As built in the PL1.8 API follow-up", plan PL1.5 acceptance 4 and risks 5 and 11, TASKS.md).
- Failure scenario closed: with `accounting` ON, a save from a client that never sends the four keys (a pre-PL1.8 cached bundle, a script, a form loaded before the switch) cleared stored HS/SST codes and TIN/SST numbers, because PL1.5 acceptance 4 ("null clears") could not tell an absent key from an explicit null. Now an absent key is never a write.
- Semantics: update with the module on: absent keeps (no assignment, no audit diff), `null` / `""` / whitespace clears, a value replaces after `TaxFieldShapes`, each key of a pair on its own; create: absent = none; module off: absent and blank are nothing, a value is the same 400 naming the field. Product import untouched (it already kept a missing column).
- Contract: OpenAPI shape and TS types UNCHANGED (properties stay optional `string | null`; the drift test is green). The client and both documents were regenerated (`api:generate` against this branch on port 5831 and Ims_Dev, `baseUrl` back to 5277, `servers` back to 5109) for the description texts only: 14 changed lines, four comment/description strings. No hand edit.
- Decisions others must respect: (1) `Optional<T>` is only for a request field where a client may omit the key AND absent must differ from null; do not spread it. (2) A request property of type `Optional<string>` must not be a constructor parameter of the JSON constructor (the OpenAPI generator cannot describe the default of a struct parameter: 500; without a default the property would become required); `TaxFieldContractTests` pins this and the `WhenWritingDefault` attribute. (3) A second `Optional<X>` needs a mapping in `OptionalSchemaTransformer` (it throws until then). (4) In C# a `string?` passed where an `Optional<string>` is expected is PRESENT (null clears), the short constructors without the fields are ABSENT; tests that need a missing key on the wire post raw JSON (see `TaxFieldPresenceTests`).
- Needs from fe-framework: nothing to do for correctness. Optional: the PL1.8a refusal ("loadedWhileOn") is now a second line of defence; keep or drop it in a web PR (not done here, web is not my lane). Needs from project-manager: tick the follow-up line after CI is green on `main`; `accounting` may be switched ON in a shared environment once this is deployed AND clients have reloaded. Needs from security-engineer: Full-tier read of the converter (an untrusted value reaches `JsonSerializer.Deserialize<T>` of string only; wrong types are a 400, never a 500; no value is echoed in an error).
- Test changes (for integration-test-engineer): `TaxFieldContractTests` updated for the new shape (requests carry `Optional<string>`, field outside the JSON constructor, `WhenWritingDefault`); new `OptionalTests` (Domain.Tests) and `TaxFieldPresenceTests` (Api.Tests, real SQL Server). `TaxReadyFieldsTests`, `TaxColumnsImportTests`, `AccountingExportTests` unchanged and green.
- Open conflicts: none.
