### 2026-10-21 be-framework -> orchestrator, security-engineer, be-architect, fe-framework, integration-test-engineer, project-manager (PL2.3b CanDisable check, switch-on guard and the deferred tests, branch `feature/pl23b-po-disable-check`)
- Produced (**Full** tier, no migration, no contract change: the OpenAPI document and the client are unchanged, the drift test stays green): (1) the real `PurchasingModuleDisableCheck` (counts Draft, Submitted and Approved orders of every warehouse by one range seek on `IX_PurchaseOrder_Status_Id`; 409 "N purchase orders are open"). (2) The TOCTOU decision (option a, narrowed to order creation): `ITransactionLocker.LockModulesSharedAsync` (same `modules` resource, `Shared` mode) is taken first by PO create (`ModuleLocks.HoldOnAsync`), then the switch is re-read from the database, then the document-sequence lock. The flip holds the exclusive form from its count to its commit, so a create either commits before the count (the flip answers 409) or waits and then finds the module off (404, nothing written). Only create adds an open order; every other transition moves an order already counted, so they take no lock. Lock order `modules` (shared) then `docseq`; the flip and both checks take no other applock; the plan's `po:{id}` lock (PL2.5) is never taken in this transaction. Mutation-proven: with the `HoldOnAsync` call commented out the in-flight tests fail. (3) Switch-ON guard: new seam `IModuleEnableCheck` (asked by `SwitchModuleHandler` only when an off module is turned on, under the `modules` lock); `PurchasingModuleEnableCheck` refuses (409, `Messages.PurchaseOrderRuleNeededToTurnOn`) while no PurchaseOrder rule is active, so the fail-open of a database the Seeder never ran against is impossible once the module is on. (4) The last active PurchaseOrder rule cannot be switched off while purchasing is on (409 `PurchaseOrderLastRule`); a rule cannot be moved onto or off `PurchaseOrder` (400 `PurchaseOrderRuleTypeFixed`). (5) A holder of `modules.manage` (by permission, the Administrator by default) may cancel ANY Draft, so a departed creator's Draft cannot block the switch-off (`PurchaseOrderActionPolicy.MayCancel`; the door is still `PurchaseOrderCancel` plus `po.read`). (6) `PurchaseOrder.IsPlainDraft` replaces `IPurchaseOrderAccess.IsRepeatedRecall`. (7) The tests deferred from #163 (role-by-state table, domain policy matrix, `canAct`, counts exclude POs, Settings rule tests, quantity rule, no-rule release, two-approver race, approve/cancel race, self-approval on, only-submitted, cancelled-is-final, one-tier forged token) plus Seeder idempotency (no new rows, a switched-off rule is not revived), the Settings gating (mutation-proven), the po.read check on cancel (Approved standard order, so `MayCancel` cannot mask it; mutation-proven), the DI discovery of enable and disable checks, a throwing check (500, flip rolled back), two checks on one module.
- Design point to know (chicken and egg): PO rules can be written only while purchasing is on (#163) and purchasing can be switched on only with an active PO rule (this PR). On a database where the Seeder never ran there are zero rules, so the module cannot be switched on and the rule cannot be added through the API. The way out is operational: run the Seeder once (`Database:MigrateOnStartup`) or insert the two `ApprovalRule` rows (DocType 5) before switching on. The go-live gate in TASKS.md stands. If you prefer, PO rules could be allowed to be written while the module is off; that would reopen #163's "hidden while off" rule, so it was not done here.
- Decisions others must respect: the enable check is a module seam like the disable check (`IModuleEnableCheck` plus `IAppService`, `ModuleCode` a catalog code, tested by DI discovery); an Administrator who must clear open orders can cancel any Draft, reject Submitted orders (a reviewer of the tier) and cancel Approved ones (a reviewer of the tier), but cannot cancel another person's Submitted order unless they submitted it.
- Owner question added (plan section 11, number 6, default: accept as is): segregation of duties covers only the submitter. A Supervisor holds `po.manage` and `document.approve`, so can edit a Draft, have a Clerk submit it, and then approve it (as with documents and `document.edit.any`). Must an editor of a Draft be barred from approving it?
- Needs from security-engineer: Full-tier read of the locks (shared against exclusive, no cycle), the enable and disable seams, and the `modules.manage` Draft cancel. Needs from be-architect: confirm the narrowed lock and the enable seam. Next role to act: orchestrator merges; then fe-framework for PL2.7 (see the PL2.3a entry: approvers filter the list by `status=Submitted`).
- Open conflicts: none.
- PL2.3b review follow-ups (same branch): (1) the last-rule guard of `UpdateApprovalRuleHandler` now runs in a transaction and takes the `modules` lock (exclusive) before it counts, because two administrators switching off the two different active rules at once would each count two (write skew); the same lock serialises it against the switch-on check; a race test proves it (mutation: without the lock call it fails). (2) `OpenPurchaseOrdersCountSpec` lists Draft, Submitted and Approved explicitly. (3) A permission-only test (ActorRights with `modules.manage` and neither `po.manage` nor ownership) pins that such an actor may cancel any Draft and nothing else, and an API test pins that the door of cancel is still 403 for `modules.manage` alone (the policy asks for `po.manage` or an approval tier). (4) `SqlTransactionLocker` lock modes are constants. Possible hardening, not done: the two in-flight race tests wait with `Task.Delay`; polling `sys.dm_tran_locks` for the WAIT on `modules` would make them deterministic.
- PLANNED next small PR (asked for by the orchestrator, after #164 merges): data-only migration `AddPurchaseOrderApprovalRules` in the style of `GrantPurchasingPermissions`: `IF NOT EXISTS (DocType = 5)` insert the two placeholder rows (RM 5,000 Standard, RM 20,000 Large) with `PublicId` and the audit columns explicit, `Down` a no-op; it removes the go-live deadlock on a database the Seeder never ran against (PO rules can be written only while purchasing is on, and it can be switched on only with an active rule). The go-live gate line in TASKS.md then adds "the owner reviews the placeholder amounts".

### 2026-10-21 be-framework -> orchestrator, be-dba, security-engineer, project-manager (PL2.3c migration `AddPurchaseOrderApprovalRules`, branch `feature/pl23c-po-rules-migration`)
- Produced (**Full** tier because it is a database change, tiny, migration MG after `AddPurchaseOrders`; no contract change, snapshot unchanged, drift test green): data-only migration `20261021090000_AddPurchaseOrderApprovalRules`. `IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM ApprovalRule WHERE DocType = 5)` it inserts the two placeholder rules (5,000.00 Standard = tier 1, 20,000.00 Large = tier 2; active; no quantity threshold) in ONE statement, with `PublicId` (NEWID), `REC_CREDATE` (SYSUTCDATETIME), `REC_CREATOR` NULL, `REC_DELETED` 0 and the revision columns NULL written explicitly (`ROW_VERSION` is generated). The migration removes that deadlock.
- Seeder change (needed because of this migration): the Seeder migrates first, so on a fresh database `ApprovalRule` is no longer empty when the default Issue rule is checked; the check is now "no rule of a document type" (`DocType <> PurchaseOrder`). Without it a fresh database would have lost the default Issue rule (5,000 Standard). The Seeder's own purchase-order block stays (it matters only where the rows were removed by hand) and writes the same values; a test deletes the pair, lets the Seeder write it and compares to the migration's text form, then runs the Seeder and the migration again and expects no duplicates.

- Tests (`AddPurchaseOrderApprovalRulesMigrationTests`): zero rules inserts two and leaves other rules alone; five existing-rule shapes insert nothing; down keeps (edited) rules and up again adds nothing; the generated script equals the runner; a fresh database equals an upgraded one; Seeder agreement and idempotency; end to end, a database with no rule: switching on is 409, then after the migration it is 200 and Settings lists the two rules.
- Needs from be-dba: a look at the statement (one INSERT guarded by NOT EXISTS on `DocType`, table is tiny). Next role to act: orchestrator merges; the owner confirms the amounts before go-live.
- Open conflicts: none.

Reversal gives the quantity back and does not reopen a Closed order (plan acceptance 7). ### 2026-10-21 be-framework -> orchestrator, project-manager, be-dba (PL2.5 review follow-up, #168)
- Done, one follow-up commit: (1) close short needs something received (`PurchaseOrderActionContext.AnythingReceived`, `PurchaseOrderActionPolicy.MayClose`); with nothing received 409 `PurchaseOrderNothingReceived` ("Cancel it instead"), `close` absent from `allowedActions`; a posted-then-reversed receipt counts as nothing; (2) a caller without po.read gets the general 409 sentences `ReceiptOrderNotOpen` / `ReceiptOverRemaining` (no PO number, status, figures, no `lines`/`code` extension) at posting and at submit, tested with a forged token; (3) `IPurchaseOrderAccess.EnsureMayClose(order, anythingReceived)` calls `MayClose`, so the offer and the guard are one rule; (4) deadlock probe now requires every response to succeed and on-hand to move by the expected total, a carton over-receipt case and an Approved-receipt case in the close-refusal theory were added; (5) doc text (allowedActions with `close`, `closeReason`, the `lines` extension) and the client regenerated; (6) TASKS PL2.5 left unticked ("built in #168, tick after CI is green on main").
- Item 7, FOR THE OWNER / be-dba: unit conversion factors are NOT immutable once used. `Product.SetConversions` and `ProductWriter` allow changing or removing a factor at any time. The order line freezes its base quantity at order time; the receipt line computes it at save. Received and over-receipt are judged in base units. Factor falls after ordering (12 to 10): receiving all 10 cartons leaves 20 pieces open, the order never auto-closes, and it shows remaining 0 cartons; a further 2-carton receipt would still post (it is within the open pieces). Factor rises (12 to 15): receiving all 10 cartons is refused as an over-receipt that reads "remaining 10". Not fixed here (catalog change, not PL2.5): proposal is to refuse a factor change or removal while the product is on an Approved order line or has movements, or to freeze the factor on the order line. Recorded in the plan.
- Open conflicts: none.

Code: `ReorderRule` (Domain, formula + the on-order status set), `IReorderReader` / `EfReorderReader` / `ReorderQuery` (one query: the shared `LowStockSets` joined to one grouped on-order sum), handler, validator, controller action, client regenerated (baseUrl 5277 and servers 5109 restored, additions only), `EndpointCatalog` row, DBA section 13 and scripts `25-seed-reorder.sql`, `25-measure-reorder.ps1`, `results-1m-reorder.csv`.
- DECISIONS OTHERS MUST RESPECT (they differ from the plan text, recorded under "As built in PL2.6" in `docs/pm/pilot-slice-2-plan.md`): (1) **on order counts Draft, Submitted and Approved** orders (the brief of this slice), not Approved only (D8 and acceptance 2 said Approved only); one constant, `ReorderRule.OnOrderStatuses`. fe-framework / PL2.10: a row leaves the list as soon as a draft covers it, so e2e `44` must not expect the row to stay after "Create purchase order" saves a draft. (6) `suggestedQty` keeps the 4-decimal quantity scale: the catalog has no countable-unit flag, so nothing rounds to whole units. (7) No cursor: page/pageSize only (bounded by one warehouse; the order is SKU).

Unit: `ReorderRuleTests` (12).

The first form (a correlated received sum per line) was P95 960 ms and 2.8 s for search; it now sums the receipts once. No index, no migration. Open for the DBA: the page statement reads about 126,000 pages (nested seeks, all in memory, 67 ms CPU); a computed "never stocked" flag on the product would remove most of it if it ever matters.

- Produced: unit conversion lock on branch `feature/unit-factor-guard` (owner decision 2026-10-21, closes PL2.5 review item 7). `Product.SetConversions` and `Product.ChangeBaseUnit` take the units in use (`ConversionLockedRule`, 400 `Messages.ConversionLocked(unit)`); `ProductConversionUsage` (Application/Catalog) finds them with one EXISTS on the ledger (index `(ProductId, WarehouseId, OccurredAt)`) and one read of the order lines by the product index (`IX_PurchaseOrderLine_ProductId`); no migration, no contract change. ### 2026-10-21 fe-framework -> orchestrator, fe-architect, qa-edge-accessibility, security-engineer, project-manager, behavior-test-engineer (PL2.7c purchase order actions, branch `feature/pl27c-po-actions`; closes PL2.7)
- Produced (web only, no API change): the action bar of `/purchase-orders/:id`. **The buttons are exactly the API's `allowedActions`** (submit, recall, approve, reject, cancel, close), never a role or a permission; unknown names are ignored. `edit` (PL2.8) and any `receive` (PL2.9) are not rendered. Reject, Cancel (reason required only for an Approved order, optional before, like the API) and Close short (offered by the API only once something was received) ask for a reason of 5 or more characters; Approve and Back to draft ask first; Submit runs at once with the success toast. Every command sends the token of the version on screen (If-Match) and is exclusive (a double click sends one); the bar has `aria-busy`, the tapped button shows the spinner, all are disabled while one runs.
- Errors: a 409 that names `document_changed` is the stale banner (Reload, buttons locked); any other 409 (cancel refused while a receipt is open, close refused) and a 400 with field errors are the API's own sentence in one alert (the interceptor does not toast a 409 on an If-Match call); a plain 400 (the order is in another state) is toasted once and the order is read again so the buttons match. 428 and 412 are not special-cased: every command sends the token and the API answers 409 for a stale one. The `over_receipt` 409 comes from posting a RECEIPT against an order (PL2.9), not from these commands, so there is nothing to show here; the alert would show its sentence as it is.
- Focus after a dialog uses the document editor's `settleFocus` workaround (banner, else the opener, else the title). The shared `ConfirmService` follow-up of TASKS.md PL1.4 (a) is still open (a small, tested change for every reason dialog at once: not done here to keep this slice to the page).
- Tests: unit: containers-purchasing is now 38 and features-purchasing 13 (new here: action matrix by `allowedActions`, reason dialogs, exclusive busy bar, stale versus other 409, wrong-state reload); e2e 42 now 20 scenarios: tiers (Supervisor on a Standard-tier order, Owner on a Large-tier one, Clerk, Warehouse staff: bar equals the API's list), self-approval not offered, submit with a double click, reject with a reason, approve, cancel with a reason, stale token banner and Reload, Close short with a reason after a partial receipt, 360 px with the bar in view, 320 px at 200% in en, ms, zh and axe light and dark with the bar. 08, 10, 11, 13, 16 and 39 green. Ims_Dev put back (purchasing OFF; every order cancelled or closed; the receipt of the part-received scenario is retired by its state in afterAll).
- Product form and the unit conversion refusal of #172 (checked, not built): the product edit screen applies server field errors to the form and a 400 without field errors is announced once by the error interceptor as a toast, so the new sentence reaches the user either way.
- Open: PL2.8 (editor: adds `edit` and the Draft actions), PL2.9 (receive against an order). Next role: qa-edge-accessibility and fe-architect for the Full-tier read of the bar (role names never used, stale versus other 409, dialogs), orchestrator merges.
- Review fixes of #173 (same branch): the store outlives the page and a sign-out, so opening an order now forgets every command's outcome (a stale 409 or a refusal of order A no longer shows on order B, or on the next user); a plain 400 or a 403 reads the order again once and forgets the failed command first (it used to re-fire for the old error when another command ran, racing its answer); Reject has its spinner; the bar is a named group ("Actions for PO-..."); e2e: A (stale) -> list -> B without a reload, the double click counts the POSTs to /submit, the clerk session is closed in finally. **The document editor has the same pattern** (`documents.resetCommands()` only in `onReload`, `openDocument` resets `detail` only): a stale 409 on document A probably shows on document B opened next. Not fixed here (its spec harness needs a case of its own): TASKS follow-up.
